As you may know, memory holds code and data for the CPU to process and the input output ports are used by the CPU to access input and output devices in the microcontroller. OVERVIEW OF THE UART PROTOCOL

We shall give a brief overview of the universal asynchronous receiver transmitter, otherwise known as the new at computers transfer data in two ways, parallel and Sirio. In parallel data transfers, often eight or more lines or wires are used to transfer data to another device in serial communication. The data is sent one bit at a time. In the past, parallel data transfer was preferred for short distances because it made TransFair multiple bits at the same time and provided higher throughput as technology advances.

![](image_rsrc1T5.jpg)

The data rate of serial communication sometimes exceed parallel communication, while parallel communication still retains the disadvantages of the size and cost of cable and connect to the crosstalk between the data lines and the difficulty of synchronizing the arrival time of data lines at longer distances also still existed in the parallel communication you ARE is one of the most common serial communication protocols. And in this project we shall explore some of the basics of the new art protocol. The data coming in at the receiving end of the data line in a serial data transfer is all zeros and ones, it is difficult to make sense of the data unless they send and receive agree on a set of rules or a protocol on how the data is packed, how many bits constitute a character and when the data begins and ends. Asynchronous Searoad data communication, like the art, is widely used for character oriented transmissions in the asynchronous method. Each character, such as ASCII characters, is packed between start and stop. This is called framing the start, but is always one bit but a stop. It can be one or two bits to start. It is always zero and the stop. It is indicated by one. For example, over here we send in the ASCII character, which has the binary number one zero zero zero zero one, which will talk about this other zero later. But there's a reason we have another zero added here. As we can see, the data is framed between the start speed and to stop it, to stop it. As we said, indicated by one and the start bit is always indicated by zero and a stop. It can be two in number, but by the stop it is always one. You can have it one bit for stop or two bits for stop. In this example, we've got two bits to stop the rightmost bit or the LSP essent. First, the LSP stands for the least significant bit in asynchronous serial communications. Peripheral chips can be programmed for data that is five, six, seven or eight bit wide.

![](image_rsrc1T6.jpg)

While in older systems ASCII characters with seven bits. The modern systems usually send Nonaka eight bits data as well. Now let's talk about parity in some systems in order to maintain data integrity. The parity bits of the character bytes are included in the data frame. This means that for each character we have a single parity bit. In addition to the start and stop bits, the parity bits may be odd, or even in the case of an odd parity, the number of data bits, including the parity bits, has an odd number of ones. Similarly, in an even parity, the total number of bits, including the parity bits, is even, for example, the ASCII character A has the binary number one zero zero zero zero one and we've got zero included to the beginning here and of course zero added here for even parity. You add chips that allow programs in the parity bits for AUD even. And in fact we can choose not to have parity at all when programming the chips and we shall see how to do this programmatically. Now we look at data transfer, the rate of data transfer in serial data communication is stated in BAPS or bits per second. Another widely used terminology for GPS is the board rate. However, the bulk rate and BPS rates are not necessarily equal. This is due to the fact that both rates are defined as the number of signal changes per second in modems. It is possible for each signal to transfer multiple bits of the data. But when we are talking about wired connections like the you at the boat rate and beeps are the same.

INTRODUCTION TO ANALOG TO DIGITAL CONVERSION

We shall give an overview of what an ADC or an analog to digital converter is. Analog to digital converters are among the most widely used devices for data acquisition. Digital computers use binary or discrete values, but in the physical world, everything is analog or continuous temperature, pressure, humidity, velocity, just a few examples of physical quantities that we deal with every day. A physical quantity is converted to electrical signals using a device called transducer, and the transducer simply converts a physical quantity to either voltage or current transducers. Used to generate electrical output are referred to US sensors, sensors for temperatures, velocity, pressure, light and many other natural physical quantities produce an output that is voltage or sometimes current. Therefore, we need an analog to digital converter to translate the analog signals to digital numbers so that the microcontroller can read and process these numbers. Now let's talk about the concept of HD You resolution and ATC has in its resolution where and can be eight, 10, 12, 16 or even 24.

![](image_rsrc1TZ.jpg)

So we hear of people saying this is 10, but as 12 with a 16 bit higher resolution Addicks provide a smaller step size, step size is the smallest change that can be detected by an ADC. This table over here shows the number of steps as well as the step size, given the number of bits the ADC has. So, for instance, an AIDS bit ADC would have two hundred and fifty six steps. And how do we know this? We simply do two to the power eight and we get 256\. And to compute the step size, we've got to take the reference voltage into account. So assuming the reference voltage is fivefold, we simply do five divided by 256 and this gives us 19 and this gives us nineteen point five, three millivolts at ten bits. ADC has a thousand and twenty four step size and the smallest change this 10 bit ADC can detect is four point eighty eight Molyvos in the same way at twelve bits. ADC has four thousand and ninety six number of steps and the smallest change this ADC can detect is a change in one point to millivolts. A sixteen bit ADC would have sixty five thousand five hundred and thirty six steps and the smallest change this ADC will be able to detect will be zero point zero seven six millivolts. The ref is an input voltage used for the reference voltage, the voltage connected to this pin. Most microcontrollers have the verafin and we can connect our own input voltage to or we can just use the same voltage as the one used by the MCU. So the voltage connected to the verafin, along with the resolution of the ADC, could determine the step size, as you can see over here. So assuming our view is fivefold and the resolution of the ADC is eight bit, then we end up with nineteen point five three volt step sizes.

INTRODUCTION TO I2C

We shall learn about the eye to see model, the eye to see stands for Inter Integrated Circuit. It is also sometimes referred to as the eye see the eye to see. But it's a simple two wire bidirectional serial communication system that is intended for communication between microcontrollers and the upper floors over short distances. Eye to see devices use only two pins for data transfer instead of the eight or more pins using traditional parallel buses. These two pins are the seal, which synchronizes the data transfer between the two chips and DDA. The CEO stands for Serial Clock and the SGA stands for zero data.

![](image_rsrc1U7.jpg)

This reduction of communication pins reduces the package size and the power consumption drastically, making them ideal for applications in which space is a major concern. These two pins, as the eight and FCL make the eye to see a two wire interface, the eye to see is sometimes called the CWI or two or two wire interface to see devices use only to bidirectional or pin Greenpeace for data communication to implement it, to see only a forty seven, only a four point seven K on pull up resistor for each of the bus lines is needed. As we can see over here, this implements a logic wide and which is needed to implement the ATC protocols. It means that if one or more devices pull the line to low or to zero level, the line stays to zero. The level of the line will be one or high only if none of the devices pulls the line low enough to see protocol. More than 50 or even 100 devices can share an eye to see the bus. Each of these devices is called a. an eye to see terminology. Each node can operate as either a master or a slave. Master is a device that generates the clock for the system. It also initiates and terminates a transmission. Slave is a node that receives the clock and is addressed by the master and I to see that both master and slave can receive or transmit data. So there are four modes of operation. These modes are the master transmitter, the master receiver, the slave transmitter and slave receiver. Notice that each node can have more than one mode of operation at different times. But it can. But it has only one mode of operation at a single time. So you can change the mode, but you cannot assume the same mode at one instance. Yeah. So let's take a look at the bit format of the HPC protocol. I see a synchronous zero protocol. Each data bit transferred on the SDK line is synchronized by a high to low pulse of clock on the echo line. According to the Itescu Protocol, the data line cannot change. When the clock line is high, it can change. Only one o'clock line is low. As we can see in this illustration, they stop and start. Conditions are the only exceptions to the rule and we shall take a look at this stop and start conditions now. So stop and start. Conditions are just as literal as they sound. They are useful. Guide in the stop and start conditions for data transfer. The bus is considered busy between each pair of stop and start conditions and no other mass that tries to take control of the bus when it is busy. If a master which has control of the bus wishes to initiate a new transfer and does not want to release the bus before starting the new transfer, it issues a new start condition between a pair of start and stop conditions. This is known as repeated starts condition or simply restarts condition. In order to see each utterance or data to be transmitted must be framed in nine bits long. The first eight bits are put on the air line by the transmitter and the ninth bit is the acknowledged by the receiver or the negative acknowledged bit. Number nine over here.

![](image_rsrc1U8.jpg)

Either the AC or the Nuk negative acknowledge the clock is always generated by the master, regardless of whether it is a transmitter receiver to acknowledge the. Transmitter releases the SD allowing during the line to walk over here in clock number nine, you can see the transmitter relays in the state line so that the receiver can pull the USDA line to indicate an acknowledgement and ask if the receiver doesn't pull the USDA line. It is considered a knock or negative acknowledgement also in order to see each byte may contain either address or data or so over here, we can notice that the static condition plus the slave address byte plus one or more data byte, plus the stock condition together form a complete data transfer.

ANALYZING THE DS1307 REAL TIME CLOCK

We are going to write a driver to interface a real time clock to our microcontroller board and the real time clock we're going to use is that one three zero seven and this is the pin out over here. You can search this on the Internet and you find its datasheet. But I'll just take you through the main key point here before we go and start programming it. So the real time clock is used in devices to provide accurate time information for applications. The real time clock is the reason why in our computer, when we turn off the computer and we turn it back on, the time is still accurate without connecting to the Internet because some computers have real time clocks and those real time clocks have external batteries that keep the time. So many systems like your PC should have a real time clock on the motherboard and the real time clock or RTC chip in the PC provides the time component of our minute second, in addition to year month and many real time clocks use external battery, which keep the time and date even when the power of the computer is off. Or do some microcontrollers come with the RTC already embedded in the chip? Many of them require us to sort of at our own RTC to it. And there is one three zero seven is a serial R2 is a serial real time clock or RTC with an eye to see Busse, which we can program. So we're going to see how to program this so that X1 and X2 are the input pins and these pins allow these one three zero seven connections to an or crystal oscillator to provide the clock source to the chip.

![](image_rsrc1U9.jpg)

We must use a thirty two point seven six eight kilohertz quiet crystal. But if you don't have the crystal, we will just experiment with this. INTRODUCTION TO SPI

We're going to learn about the serial peripheral interface, which is known as spy for Short. Also, another name for this serial peripheral interface is the Synchronoss serial interface, or SSI, for short. So if you see a spy denoted, tell us. It's the same thing. Spy allows microcontrollers to communicate synchronously with peripheral devices and other microcontrollers. We can operate the spy system as either master or slave. Before we talk more about the spy. Let's compare it to the new art and see the key differences that you add allows asynchronous communication. And that is why it is called a universal asynchronous receiver transmitter. The spy allows synchronized communication.

![](image_rsrc1UU.jpg)

This means that a device is communicating whether you are to operate at the same frequency, which we call the portrait, but have separately but have separate clocks with you at the clock. Signal is not included in the interface. Cable between devices can communicate with each other as long as the clock frequencies or both are within plus or minus five percent of each other. On the other hand, two devices communicating with spies operate from the same source. Typically, the master device creates that clock and the slave device uses the clock to latch data in and send data out. The spy typically consists of four wires or four pins. These are the as the eye, the steel, the SC, L.K. and Dessy in many chips, the SDI, SD, or SLK and C signals are alternatively named as the Mausi, the missile, the Sink and the Espin. So if you find Morsey missile, Sikhi SS is the same as SDI as the or C L.K. and see if there is also a popular standard called the three wire spy in a three way spy interface. Plus we have the SLK and the C and only a single pin for data transfer. The spy for wire pass can become a three wire interface when the SDI and DST or data pins are tied together. However, there are some major differences between the standard spy for wire, which we are using, and the three wire device in the data transfer protocol. So for this reason, a device must support three wire protocols internally in order to be used as a three wire device.

![](image_rsrc1UV.jpg)

Many devices support both the spy for wire and the three wire protocol. No, let's talk about how the spy works. So the spy consists of two shift registers, one for the master and the other one for the slave. Also, there is a clock generator in the master side that generates the clock for the two shift registers. As we can see over here, zero out of the master shift register is connected to the zero in pin of the slave shift register by the Mausi line. And Moses stands for Master Out Slave in and the zero in pin of the Master Shift Register is connected to the zero output of the slave ship to register by the misalign. And measle means master in Slave Out, the master clock generator provides clock to both master and slave shift registers, the clock input of the shift registers can be falling or rising. Triggered in S.P.I.

![](image_rsrc1UW.jpg)

The shift registers are eight bits long. This means that after eight o'clock pulses, the content of the two shift registers are interchanged. When the master wants to send a bit of data, it replaces the byte in the shift. It places the bytes in its shift register and generates eight clock pulses after eight clock pulses. Debate is transmitted to the slave ship to register. When the master wants to receive a bit of data, the slave side should place the byte in its shift to register. And after eight o'clock policies, the data will be received by the master shift register. We shall see how this works programmatically next. Let's take a look at the spy clock polarity, as we learned earlier. And you are to communicate. The transmitter and a receiver must agree on a clock frequency, which we call the rate. But the master and slave must agree on the clock polarity and the face of the clock.
